Design a water-recovery experiment

CL-0072 · VisionariesAI Labs Private Limited · Virtual practical

Aim

Design and compare reference water-recovery experiments. Relate collected feed water to source heating, heat loss, condenser capacity, escaped vapour and separate cooling-water demand.

Principle and apparatus

Round-bottom source flask, chosen delivered heater, temperature probe, separate-jacket condenser, coolant inlet/outlet, open-vented collection path, ideal insulated hot-water collector and virtual timer/balance. Source warming follows a lumped heat balance. Finite boiling requires delivered power above the source loss at100°C. Net boiling heat supplies latent evaporation; condenser cooling capacity limits captured vapour. Feed remaining, recovered and escaped are separate parts of the same original feed. Cooling-water throughput is a separate stream. Condensate is hot100°C liquid in this latent-only reference; no purity, contaminant removal or safe-water conclusion follows.

Procedure

  1. Record original feed mass, delivered power, heat-loss coefficient, coolant flow/inlet temperature and a collection target. Predict whether the design can boil and capture enough water.
  2. Record the initial state, warm-up temperature and predicted boiling time. Keep all other controls fixed when testing one design change.
  3. During boiling, record remaining feed, collected water, escaped water and instantaneous cooling flow. Compare condenser capacity with boiling heat supply.
  4. Record delivered heat, ambient loss, sensible heat, escaped-vapour enthalpy and coolant heat gain. Check the complete reference heat account.
  5. Observe automatic heater cutoff with10g liquid remaining and subsequent source cooling. Record hot collected volume separately from mass.
  6. Repeat no cooling, stronger cooling, higher heater power and a heater/loss combination that cannot reach boiling. Compare recovery, thermal input per gram, coolant use per gram and target feasibility.
  7. Write the result and conclusion from recorded trials. Clearly distinguish current observations from future design predictions, and state the assumptions.

Prediction and experimental plan

<design> More cooling should recover more feed; compare external coolant demand separately.

Formula substitution and worked calculations

Trial 1: warming

  1. Reference temperature rise

    ΔT = Tb−Ta

    Substitution: 100−20

    Result: 80.000000 K; same numerical temperature difference as80°C

  2. Initial feed and vessel heat capacity

    C₀ = m₀cp+Cv

    Substitution: 100.000000 × 4.18+40

    Result: 458.000000 J/K

  3. Source heat loss at boiling

    Ploss,b = UA ΔT

    Substitution: 0.5 ×80

    Result: 40.000000 W

  4. Available power for boiling

    Pb = max(0,P−Ploss,b)

    Substitution: max(0,160.000000−40.000000)

    Result: 120.000000 W; finite boiling also requires P>Ploss,b

  5. Predicted boiling start

    tb = −C₀/UA ln(1−UAΔT/P)

    Substitution: −458.000000/0.5 ×ln(1−40.000000/160.000000)

    Result: 263.516778 s; prediction, not elapsed measured time

  6. Elapsed warming interval

    twarm = min(t,tb)

    Substitution: min(0,263.516778)

    Result: 0 s

  7. Temperature at the end of the warming interval

    Twarm = Ta+(P/UA)[1−exp(−UA twarm/C₀)]

    Substitution: 20+(160.000000/0.5)[1−exp(−0.5 × 0/458.000000)]

    Result: 20.000000 °C

  8. Feed available before automatic cutoff

    mavailable = m₀−mreserve

    Substitution: 100.000000−10

    Result: 90.000000 g; chosen model reserve

  9. Reference vapour production rate

    ṁvap = Pb/L

    Substitution: 120.000000/2256.4

    Result: 0.05318205992 g/s during boiling

  10. Predicted boiling duration

    Δtb = (m₀−mreserve)/ṁvap

    Substitution: (100.000000−10)/0.05318205992

    Result: 1692.300000 s

  11. Predicted automatic heater cutoff

    tc = tb+Δtb

    Substitution: 263.516778+1692.300000

    Result: 1955.816778 s; retains10g liquid

  12. Actual boiling interval so far

    tboil = min(max(0,t−tb),Δtb)

    Substitution: min(max(0,0−263.516778),1692.300000)

    Result: 0 s

  13. Active heater time

    theater = min(t,tc)

    Substitution: min(0,1955.816778)

    Result: 0 s

  14. Evaporated feed

    mevap = min(m₀−mreserve,ṁvap tboil)

    Substitution: min(100.000000−10,0.05318205992 × 0)

    Result: 0 g; not all necessarily recovered

  15. Feed remaining in source

    msource = m₀−mevap

    Substitution: 100.000000−0

    Result: 100.000000 g

  16. Remaining source heat capacity

    Csource = msource cp+Cv

    Substitution: 100.000000 × 4.18+40

    Result: 458.000000 J/K

  17. Elapsed post-cutoff cooling

    tcool = max(0,t−tc)

    Substitution: max(0,0−1955.816778)

    Result: 0 s

  18. Current source temperature

    T = Twarm

    Substitution: 20.000000

    Result: 20.000000 °C; no superheating solver

  19. Allowed coolant temperature rise

    ΔTcool = Tout,max−Tin

    Substitution: 35−20.000000

    Result: 15.000000 K

  20. Ideal condenser latent-heat capacity

    Q̇cap = ṁcool cp ΔTcool

    Substitution: 1.500000 × 4.18 × (35−20.000000)

    Result: 94.050000 W; chosen outlet design limit

  21. Predicted vapour capture fraction

    f = min(1,Q̇cap/Pb)

    Substitution: min(1,94.050000/120.000000)

    Result: 0.78375 fraction; design prediction

  22. Recovered feed water

    mrec = f mevap

    Substitution: 0.78375 × 0

    Result: 0 g

  23. Escaped feed water

    mesc = mevap−mrec

    Substitution: 0−0

    Result: 0 g; assigned to invisible vapour leaving the system

  24. Primary-feed conservation

    m₀ = msource+mrec+mesc

    Substitution: 100.000000+0+0

    Result: 100.000000 g; external coolant excluded

  25. Temperature-dependent liquid-density reference

    ρsource = ρ20+(ρ100−ρ20)(T−20)/80

    Substitution: 0.99816+(0.95835−0.99816)(20.000000−20)/80

    Result: 0.99816 g/mL; linear reference approximation

  26. Source liquid volume

    Vsource = msource/ρsource

    Substitution: 100.000000/0.99816

    Result: 100.184339 mL

  27. Hot collected liquid volume

    Vrec = mrec/ρ100

    Substitution: 0/0.95835

    Result: 0 mL; collector is hot, not subcooled

  28. Delivered thermal input

    Qin = P theater

    Substitution: 160.000000 × 0

    Result: 0 J; delivered heat, not electrical consumption

  29. Delivered thermal input in watt-hours

    Qin,Wh = Qin/3600

    Substitution: 0/3600

    Result: 0 Wh of delivered heat

  30. Ambient loss during warming

    Qloss,warm = Ptwarm−C₀(Twarm−Ta)

    Substitution: 160.000000 × 0−458.000000(20.000000−20)

    Result: 0 J

  31. Ambient loss during boiling

    Qloss,boil = UA ΔT tboil

    Substitution: 0.5 ×80 × 0

    Result: 0 J

  32. Ambient loss after cutoff

    Qloss,cool = 0

    Substitution: No post-cutoff cooling has elapsed

    Result: 0 J

  33. Total ambient heat loss

    Qloss = Qloss,warm+Qloss,boil+Qloss,cool

    Substitution: 0+0+0

    Result: 0 J

  34. Source sensible heat reference

    Hsource = Csource(T−Ta)

    Substitution: 458.000000(20.000000−20)

    Result: 0 J

  35. Hot collected-water sensible heat

    Hrec = mrec cp(Tb−Ta)

    Substitution: 0 × 4.18 ×80

    Result: 0 J; ideal insulated collector

  36. Escaped-vapour enthalpy reference

    Hesc = mesc[cp(Tb−Ta)+L]

    Substitution: 0[4.18 ×80+2256.4]

    Result: 0 J

  37. Latent heat transferred to coolant

    Qcool = mrec L

    Substitution: 0 × 2256.4

    Result: 0 J; condensate subcooling not included

  38. Complete reference heat balance

    Qaccounted = Hsource+Hrec+Hesc+Qcool+Qloss

    Substitution: 0+0+0+0+0

    Result: 0 J; equals delivered thermal input

  39. External cooling-water throughput

    mcool = ṁcool tboil

    Substitution: 1.500000 × 0

    Result: 0 g; a separate stream, not recovered feed

  40. Mean cooling-water outlet temperature

    No cooling water has passed

    Substitution: Do not divide by zero

    Result: Not applicable: no cooling water has passed

  41. Current cooling-water outlet temperature

    No current cooling flow

    Substitution: Pump is off or no cooling flow was selected

    Result: Not applicable: no current cooling flow

  42. Collected liquid temperature reference

    No recovered liquid yet

    Substitution: An empty collector has no liquid temperature reading

    Result: Not applicable: no recovered liquid yet

  43. Maximum capture-limited collection

    mrec,max = (m₀−mreserve)f

    Substitution: (100.000000−10) × 0.78375

    Result: 70.537500 g; design prediction

  44. Predicted target-collection time

    ttarget = tb+mtarget/(ṁvap f)

    Substitution: 263.516778+50.000000/(0.05318205992 × 0.78375)

    Result: 1463.091473 s; prediction inside2400s run

  45. Current feed-water recovery

    R =100 mrec/m₀

    Substitution: 100 × 0/100.000000

    Result: 0 %

  46. Delivered heat per recovered gram

    No recovered mass yet

    Substitution: Do not divide by zero

    Result: Not applicable: no recovered liquid yet

  47. Coolant demand per recovered gram

    No recovered mass yet

    Substitution: Do not divide by zero

    Result: Not applicable: no recovered liquid yet

  48. Collection if once-through coolant is discarded

    Δmcollection = mrec−mcool

    Substitution: 0−0

    Result: 0 g; conditional operational comparison, not water destruction

  49. Current generated-vapour rate

    ṁvap,current = 0

    Substitution: Source is not currently boiling

    Result: 0 g/s

  50. Current recovered-water rate

    ṁrec,current = f ṁvap,current

    Substitution: 0.78375 × 0

    Result: 0 g/s

  51. Current escaped-water rate

    ṁesc,current = ṁvap,current−ṁrec,current

    Substitution: 0−0

    Result: 0 g/s

Trial 2: warming

  1. Reference temperature rise

    ΔT = Tb−Ta

    Substitution: 100−20

    Result: 80.000000 K; same numerical temperature difference as80°C

  2. Initial feed and vessel heat capacity

    C₀ = m₀cp+Cv

    Substitution: 100.000000 × 4.18+40

    Result: 458.000000 J/K

  3. Source heat loss at boiling

    Ploss,b = UA ΔT

    Substitution: 0.5 ×80

    Result: 40.000000 W

  4. Available power for boiling

    Pb = max(0,P−Ploss,b)

    Substitution: max(0,160.000000−40.000000)

    Result: 120.000000 W; finite boiling also requires P>Ploss,b

  5. Predicted boiling start

    tb = −C₀/UA ln(1−UAΔT/P)

    Substitution: −458.000000/0.5 ×ln(1−40.000000/160.000000)

    Result: 263.516778 s; prediction, not elapsed measured time

  6. Elapsed warming interval

    twarm = min(t,tb)

    Substitution: min(100.000000,263.516778)

    Result: 100.000000 s

  7. Temperature at the end of the warming interval

    Twarm = Ta+(P/UA)[1−exp(−UA twarm/C₀)]

    Substitution: 20+(160.000000/0.5)[1−exp(−0.5 × 100.000000/458.000000)]

    Result: 53.095132 °C

  8. Feed available before automatic cutoff

    mavailable = m₀−mreserve

    Substitution: 100.000000−10

    Result: 263.516778 s

  7. Temperature at the end of the warming interval

    Twarm = Ta+(P/UA)[1−exp(−UA twarm/C₀)]

    Substitution: 20+(160.000000/0.5)[1−exp(−0.5 × 263.516778/458.000000)]

    Result: 100.000000 °C

    mavailable = m₀−mreserve

    Substitution: max(0,800.000000−1955.816778)

    Result: 0 s

  18. Current source temperature

    T = Tb

    Substitution: Reference boiling temperature100

  19. Allowed coolant temperature rise

    ΔTcool = Tout,max−Tin

    Substitution: 35−20.000000

    Result: 15.000000 K

  20. Ideal condenser latent-heat capacity

    Q̇cap = ṁcool cp ΔTcool

    Substitution: 1.500000 × 4.18 × (35−20.000000)

    f = min(1,Q̇cap/Pb)

    Substitution: 71.468717+22.361393+6.169890

    Result: 100.000000 g; external coolant excluded

    Substitution: 22.361393 × 2256.4

    Result: 128000.000000 J; equals delivered thermal input

    Substitution: 1.500000 × 536.483222

    qrec = Qin/mrec

    Substitution: 256000.000000/55.706544

Trial 8: cooling

  1. Reference temperature rise

    ΔT = Tb−Ta

    Substitution: 100−20

    Result: 80.000000 K; same numerical temperature difference as80°C

  2. Initial feed and vessel heat capacity

    C₀ = m₀cp+Cv

    Substitution: 100.000000 × 4.18+40

    Result: 458.000000 J/K

  3. Source heat loss at boiling

    Ploss,b = UA ΔT

    Substitution: 0.5 ×80

    Result: 40.000000 W

  4. Available power for boiling

    Pb = max(0,P−Ploss,b)

    Substitution: max(0,160.000000−40.000000)

    Result: 120.000000 W; finite boiling also requires P>Ploss,b

  5. Predicted boiling start

    tb = −C₀/UA ln(1−UAΔT/P)

    Substitution: −458.000000/0.5 ×ln(1−40.000000/160.000000)

    Result: 263.516778 s; prediction, not elapsed measured time

  6. Elapsed warming interval

    twarm = min(t,tb)

    Substitution: min(2000.000000,263.516778)

    Result: 263.516778 s

  7. Temperature at the end of the warming interval

    Twarm = Ta+(P/UA)[1−exp(−UA twarm/C₀)]

    Substitution: 20+(160.000000/0.5)[1−exp(−0.5 × 263.516778/458.000000)]

    Result: 100.000000 °C

  8. Feed available before automatic cutoff

    mavailable = m₀−mreserve

    Substitution: 100.000000−10

    Result: 90.000000 g; chosen model reserve

  9. Reference vapour production rate

    ṁvap = Pb/L

    Substitution: 120.000000/2256.4

    Result: 0.05318205992 g/s during boiling

  10. Predicted boiling duration

    Δtb = (m₀−mreserve)/ṁvap

    Substitution: (100.000000−10)/0.05318205992

    Result: 1692.300000 s

  11. Predicted automatic heater cutoff

    tc = tb+Δtb

    Substitution: 263.516778+1692.300000

    Result: 1955.816778 s; retains10g liquid

  12. Actual boiling interval so far

    tboil = min(max(0,t−tb),Δtb)

    Substitution: min(max(0,2000.000000−263.516778),1692.300000)

    Result: 1692.300000 s

  13. Active heater time

    theater = min(t,tc)

    Substitution: min(2000.000000,1955.816778)

    Result: 1955.816778 s

  14. Evaporated feed

    mevap = min(m₀−mreserve,ṁvap tboil)

    Substitution: min(100.000000−10,0.05318205992 × 1692.300000)

    Result: 90.000000 g; not all necessarily recovered

  15. Feed remaining in source

    msource = m₀−mevap

    Substitution: 100.000000−90.000000

    Result: 10.000000 g

  16. Remaining source heat capacity

    Csource = msource cp+Cv

    Substitution: 10.000000 × 4.18+40

    Result: 81.800000 J/K

  17. Elapsed post-cutoff cooling

    tcool = max(0,t−tc)

    Substitution: max(0,2000.000000−1955.816778)

    Result: 44.183222 s

  18. Current source temperature

    T = Ta+ΔT exp(−UA tcool/Csource)

    Substitution: 20+80exp(−0.5 × 44.183222/81.800000)

    Result: 81.066171 °C; no superheating solver

  19. Allowed coolant temperature rise

    ΔTcool = Tout,max−Tin

    Substitution: 35−20.000000

    Result: 15.000000 K

  20. Ideal condenser latent-heat capacity

    Q̇cap = ṁcool cp ΔTcool

    Substitution: 1.500000 × 4.18 × (35−20.000000)

    Result: 94.050000 W; chosen outlet design limit

  21. Predicted vapour capture fraction

    f = min(1,Q̇cap/Pb)

    Substitution: min(1,94.050000/120.000000)

    Result: 0.78375 fraction; design prediction

  22. Recovered feed water

    mrec = f mevap

    Substitution: 0.78375 × 90.000000

    Result: 70.537500 g

  23. Escaped feed water

    mesc = mevap−mrec

    Substitution: 90.000000−70.537500

    Result: 19.462500 g; assigned to invisible vapour leaving the system

  24. Primary-feed conservation

    m₀ = msource+mrec+mesc

    Substitution: 10.000000+70.537500+19.462500

    Result: 100.000000 g; external coolant excluded

  25. Temperature-dependent liquid-density reference

    ρsource = ρ20+(ρ100−ρ20)(T−20)/80

    Substitution: 0.99816+(0.95835−0.99816)(81.066171−20)/80

    Result: 0.9677719468 g/mL; linear reference approximation

    Vsource = msource/ρsource

    Substitution: 10.000000/0.9677719468

    Result: 10.333013 mL

  27. Hot collected liquid volume

    Vrec = mrec/ρ100

    Substitution: 70.537500/0.95835

    Result: 73.603068 mL; collector is hot, not subcooled

  28. Delivered thermal input

    Qin = P theater

    Substitution: 160.000000 × 1955.816778

    Result: 312930.684539 J; delivered heat, not electrical consumption

  29. Delivered thermal input in watt-hours

    Qin,Wh = Qin/3600

    Substitution: 312930.684539/3600

  30. Ambient loss during warming

  34. Source sensible heat reference

    Hsource = Csource(T−Ta)

    Substitution: 81.800000(81.066171−20)

    Substitution: 19.462500[4.18 ×80+2256.4]

    Substitution: 70.537500 × 2256.4

    Result: 312930.684539 J; equals delivered thermal input

    mcool = ṁcool tboil

    Substitution: 1.500000 × 1692.300000

    Result: 2538.450000 g; a separate stream, not recovered feed

    Result: 1.39382733e-5 J; ideal insulated collector

  36. Escaped-vapour enthalpy reference

    Hesc = mesc[cp(Tb−Ta)+L]

    Substitution: 1.15006204e-8[4.18 ×80+2256.4]

    Result: 2.97958074e-5 J

  37. Latent heat transferred to coolant

    Qcool = mrec L

    Substitution: 4.16814394e-8 × 2256.4

    Result: 9.40499998e-5 J; condensate subcooling not included

  38. Complete reference heat balance

    Qaccounted = Hsource+Hrec+Hesc+Qcool+Qloss

    Substitution: 36639.999982+1.39382733e-5+2.97958074e-5+9.40499998e-5+5522.684579

    Result: 42162.684699 J; equals delivered thermal input

  39. External cooling-water throughput

    mcool = ṁcool tboil

    Substitution: 1.500000 × 9.99999997e-7

    Result: 1.50000000e-6 g; a separate stream, not recovered feed

  40. Mean cooling-water outlet temperature

    Tout,mean = Tin+Qcool/(mcool cp)

    Substitution: 20.000000+9.40499998e-5/(1.50000000e-6 × 4.18)

    Result: 35.000000 °C; cumulative mean

  41. Current cooling-water outlet temperature

    Tout = Tin+ṁrec L/(ṁcool cp)

    Substitution: 20.000000+0.04168143946 × 2256.4/(1.500000 × 4.18)

    Result: 35.000000 °C; current flowing stream

  42. Collected liquid temperature reference

    Trec = Tb for latent-only condensation

    Substitution: Ideal insulated collector; no condensate subcooling

    Result: 100.000000 °C; not cool or proven safe water

  43. Maximum capture-limited collection

    mrec,max = (m₀−mreserve)f

    Substitution: (100.000000−10) × 0.78375

    Result: 70.537500 g; design prediction

  44. Predicted target-collection time

    ttarget = tb+mtarget/(ṁvap f)

    Substitution: 263.516778+50.000000/(0.05318205992 × 0.78375)

    Result: 1463.091473 s; prediction inside2400s run

Trial 11: cooling

  1. Reference temperature rise

    ΔT = Tb−Ta

    Substitution: 100−20

    Result: 80.000000 K; same numerical temperature difference as80°C

  2. Initial feed and vessel heat capacity

    C₀ = m₀cp+Cv

    Substitution: 100.000000 × 4.18+40

    Result: 458.000000 J/K

  3. Source heat loss at boiling

    Ploss,b = UA ΔT

    Substitution: 0.5 ×80

    Result: 40.000000 W

  4. Available power for boiling

    Pb = max(0,P−Ploss,b)

    Substitution: max(0,160.000000−40.000000)

    Result: 120.000000 W; finite boiling also requires P>Ploss,b

  5. Predicted boiling start

    tb = −C₀/UA ln(1−UAΔT/P)

    Substitution: −458.000000/0.5 ×ln(1−40.000000/160.000000)

    Result: 263.516778 s; prediction, not elapsed measured time

  6. Elapsed warming interval

    twarm = min(t,tb)

    Substitution: min(1955.816779,263.516778)

    Result: 263.516778 s

  7. Temperature at the end of the warming interval

    Twarm = Ta+(P/UA)[1−exp(−UA twarm/C₀)]

    Substitution: 20+(160.000000/0.5)[1−exp(−0.5 × 263.516778/458.000000)]

    Result: 100.000000 °C

  8. Feed available before automatic cutoff

    mavailable = m₀−mreserve

    Substitution: 100.000000−10

    Result: 90.000000 g; chosen model reserve

  9. Reference vapour production rate

    ṁvap = Pb/L

    Substitution: 120.000000/2256.4

    Result: 0.05318205992 g/s during boiling

  10. Predicted boiling duration

    Δtb = (m₀−mreserve)/ṁvap

    Substitution: (100.000000−10)/0.05318205992

    Result: 1692.300000 s

  11. Predicted automatic heater cutoff

    tc = tb+Δtb

    Substitution: 263.516778+1692.300000

    Result: 1955.816778 s; retains10g liquid

  12. Actual boiling interval so far

    tboil = min(max(0,t−tb),Δtb)

    Substitution: min(max(0,1955.816779−263.516778),1692.300000)

    Result: 1692.300000 s

  13. Active heater time

    theater = min(t,tc)

    Substitution: min(1955.816779,1955.816778)

    Result: 1955.816778 s

  14. Evaporated feed

    Substitution: min(100.000000−10,0.05318205992 × 1692.300000)

    Result: 90.000000 g; not all necessarily recovered

  15. Feed remaining in source

    Substitution: 100.000000−90.000000

    Result: 10.000000 g

    Csource = msource cp+Cv

    Substitution: 10.000000 × 4.18+40

    Result: 81.800000 J/K

  17. Elapsed post-cutoff cooling

    Substitution: max(0,1955.816779−1955.816778)

    Result: 1.00000034e-6 s

    Substitution: 20+80exp(−0.5 × 1.00000034e-6/81.800000)

    Result: 100.000000 °C; no superheating solver

    Substitution: 35−20.000000

    Result: 15.000000 K

Trial 14: boiling

  1. Reference temperature rise

    ΔT = Tb−Ta

    Substitution: 100−20

    Result: 80.000000 K; same numerical temperature difference as80°C

  2. Initial feed and vessel heat capacity

    C₀ = m₀cp+Cv

    Substitution: 100.000000 × 4.18+40

    Result: 458.000000 J/K

  3. Source heat loss at boiling

    Ploss,b = UA ΔT

    Substitution: 0.5 ×80

    Result: 40.000000 W

    Substitution: max(0,160.000000−40.000000)

Trial 35: boiling

  1. Reference temperature rise

    ΔT = Tb−Ta

    Substitution: 100−20

    Result: 80.000000 K; same numerical temperature difference as80°C

    C₀ = m₀cp+Cv

    Substitution: 100.000000 × 4.18+40

    Result: 458.000000 J/K

  3. Source heat loss at boiling

    Ploss,b = UA ΔT

    Substitution: 0.5 ×80

    Result: 40.000000 W

  4. Available power for boiling

    Pb = max(0,P−Ploss,b)

    Substitution: max(0,60.000000−40.000000)

    Result: 20.000000 W; finite boiling also requires P>Ploss,b

Result and conclusion

Use recorded feed and heat accounts to justify the target. Increased heater power with unchanged cooling may reduce total capture. Ideal hot collected water is not evidence of safe water.

Report collected mass and percent, escaped feed, delivered thermal input per recovered gram, external coolant per recovered gram and whether the collection target was reached. A faster heater may overwhelm the same condenser. More once-through cooling can improve capture while using much more cooling water. Discarded coolant is not destroyed water; a return/recycling design changes that comparison. These trials do not establish water purity or safe consumption.

Precautions and limitations

This is a virtual investigation; real hot-water/heating work requires teacher supervision and suitable apparatus. Pure reference feed, fixed100°C boiling at101.42kPa reference pressure, constant cp4.18J/(gK), latent heat2256.4J/g and chosen40J/K vessel capacity. Effective linear source heat loss, uniform liquid temperature and model heater cutoff with10g reserve. Ideal quasi-steady condenser removes latent heat only; negligible exchanger storage, interlocked cooling flow, no subcooling or collector heat loss/ongoing evaporation. Liquid volume uses linearly interpolated saturated-liquid density references, not a full thermophysical equation of state. No pressure/altitude, calibrated flow/heat transfer, contamination, entrainment or potable-water validation. Conduit arrows/markers show transfer direction, not extra fluid inventory or resolved flow velocity. Editorial class placement; detailed heat/flow formulas are teacher extensions.
